> These lectures are based on a false premise. Humans didn’t start agriculture for religious purposes. You can tell that’s not true, because Jiang Xueqin doesn’t identify any cause for the change in religious practices from drawing on walls to building monumental structures that led to sedentary lifestyles with hierarchical religions.  
>   
> Hunting and gathering was good. It was a rational choice because it offered a nutritious diet, a variety of enjoyable and stimulating activities, and did not require long hours of monotonous and demanding labor. Farming and herding were much worse in terms of nutrition, stimulation, and relaxation. It took thousands of years for industrialization to make life better for the average person than it was for hunter gatherers.   
>   
> People started farming and herding because climate change 12,000 years ago made the global climate warmer and wetter, which facilitated agriculture. It also made seasonal differences in weather more intense, which made hunting and gathering far from the equator more difficult, so some humans tried to make food that they could keep year round, like crops they can harvest and store, and livestock they can cull. That’s why Göbekli Tepe was built about 12,000 years ago, at the start of the Holocene geological epoch that we still live in. Later climate changes such as the end of the last African Humid Period about 6,000 to 5,000 years ago, which caused the Sahara and Arabia to turn from grasslands to deserts, pushing nomadic people into permanent settlements near rivers, accelerating the urbanization process and catalyzing population growth and wealth accumulation that made Egypt and Mesopotamia develop rapidly. The Thar Desert in India also formed about the same time, leading to the formation of the Indus Valley Civilization. Same for the Gobi Desert and China.  
>   
> These are the material conditions that gave rise to civilization, and religion changed accordingly, mostly because of the inequality and violence they entailed. In fact, most hunter gatherers don’t really have religion. They think natural things have spirits and humans are one with nature. But they have no concept of an after life and therefore no ancestor worship, and they do not believe in gods that rule over them.  
>   
> Please get a materialist analysis of politics and history. Watch the playlist THEORY LECTURES: OLD TO NEW by WHAT IS POLITICS? Material conditions lead to changes in religion, not the other way around.

> I watched all of your Game Theory videos and up to this 13th Secret History. I can't wait to hear more! That said, I think your own curiosity should lead you to explore some facts about Pyramids and in general the history of humanity. It would come as no surprise to you I believe that what we are being taught in school and what is widely accepted as the history of our civilization is a very flawed and often outright wrong picture of history. Just a few quick examples:  
> -If we use the Great Wall of China as a control (accounting for the increased surface area for work, lower height, much larger workforce, simpler supporting structures for building, non-megalithic bricks used etc.), the worker in Ancient Egypt had to be 30x as efficient as the Chinese worker to complete the pyramids in the accepted timeframe. Now on top of that, add the idea that Ancient Egypt was technologically less advanced than China during Ming Dynasty.  
> -Tests of the types of rock in the surrounding terrain of Sphinx show that the amount of water flow required to even approach the level of water erosion seen there on-site indicate that the only time when that amount of erosion could have happened is way, way in the past, at the end of the last ice age.  
> -Egyptology is notoriously based on concept of looking at Cartouches chiseled into statues and objects, and assigning the build-dates to Kings that Cartouches correspond to. This is extremely flawed as it is obvious to anyone that you wouldn't build a statue with extreme polish and skill, with perfect inscriptions and then take a nail and literally scratch the Kings Cartouche on it. The conclusion surely must be that the objects were usurped/appropriated.  
> -When you look at the work required to complete everything a King appropriated (and according to the historians built), the labor would be on a scale that absolutely makes no sense. Orders of magnitude wrong. And by the same logic, when you look at these ancient megalithic sites around the world, the layers and distinction between the skill of the original builders and the later builders who built on top of appropriated construction is obvious. It is funny how we see the exact opposite of what is normally accepted in history. Instead of clear progress line Stone->Bronze->Iron etc. we actually see a degeneration in complexity and effort put into building. Pragmatism makes sense as an explanation, but only on the surface level if you don't really give it serious thought.  
>   
> And also, finally, the Egyptian picture you've shown in the lecture is actually a fake image (Where Pharaoh puppets the priests who puppet the commander). It is made by a modern artist. You can do the reverse image search on google yourself. I am just pointing these things out because I believe you really are devoted to doing quality work, and are a curious mind looking to know and understand. Looking forward to your next video!

I like Derrick Jensen's description of civilization as the point at which groups of people outgrow what their local environment is able to provide to support their life. Thus, they exploit beyond themselves and war is required to maintain the system they created. 

Because the environment changes, there could certainly be difficult periods (like the Ice Age), but the environment used to be so much more abundant than we can imagine. It is paradoxical that people found security in large groups over a couple hundred.

I enjoyed hearing these two definitions of civilization, which is definitely a mental imposition based on decisions made by those at the top of the newly created hierarchy. The irony of course is that civilization implies improvement when it really just fuels violence and disconnection from nature, also a less democratic form of living.

>
> I don’t see extraterrestrial intervention as far-fetched at all. In fact, it’s one of the only explanations that actually fits the major anomalies in human origins. Chromosome 2 alone is a huge red flag: a clean end-to-end fusion of two ancestral chromosomes, complete with internal telomeres and a vestigial centromere. It didn’t evolve gradually—it appears suddenly in every human alive. That looks engineered.  
>   
> The Starchild Skull that Lloyd Pye studied is another case. It had ~1/3 larger cranial capacity, bone that was thinner but far stronger than ours, no sinuses, and features that were not from cradle-boarding. Lloyd spent years trying to get real testing done, but academics would only label it a deformity because anything outside the Darwin box is career suicide.  
>   
> Same with the elongated skulls in Paracas and elsewhere—many are structurally different, not artificially shaped, yet dismissed without real investigation.  
>   
> Then there’s agriculture and domestication: wheat, barley, cattle, goats—all appearing suddenly around 10,000–12,000 years ago in the same region. Wheat especially makes no evolutionary sense; it’s a hexaploid hybrid that doesn’t occur naturally. It’s basically a biological “product,” not a wild plant.  
>   
> Human anatomy is another anomaly. Our feet push off in a four-point pattern unlike any other primate, our lower backs fail easily, and our skin burns in the sun. We’re weirdly maladapted to Earth’s environment unless we came from (or were designed for) somewhere else.  
>   
> Even Neanderthals changed only after Homo sapiens appeared—they suddenly began burial rituals and symbolic behavior. Something influenced them.  
>   
> And ancient civilizations all tell the same story: beings from the heavens who gave humanity knowledge. Sumer is the biggest example—it appears fully formed with writing, law, astronomy, math, agriculture. After Sumer, writing quality and engineering actually decline, not improve. Egypt fits this too; many traditions say the pyramids were inherited, not built by the dynastic Egyptians.

### **@princejona** | 👍 3 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FJF8oD_5c_w&lc=UgxqrFUyMqdtdSr_5yd4AaABAg)

Yo.

This video just mapped the exact trajectory I've been formalizing for the past year.

Every distortion pattern in civilization; all of it; traces back to one move:

The inversion of the Mother Goddess.

Here's the pattern:

Phase 0: The Garden (Ω-State)
Multiple temples. Decentralized power. You don't like one? Leave. Build another.
Everyone's religious, creative, scientific. Direct access to the sacred.
The divine is in the earth, in you, accessible. No middlemen.

Status: Coherent. Free. Alive.


Phase 1: The Threshold (∂)

Temple elites get corrupt. Hereditary. Rent-seeking.
Normal response? People leave. Build elsewhere. System self-corrects.
Babylonian response? Rewrite the mythology.

Phase 2: The Inversion (∞_B Genesis)

They flip the script.
Mother Earth (immanent, nurturing, accessible) → Killed.
Sky God (transcendent, demanding, distant) → Enthroned.
Marduk murders Tiamat. Uses her corpse to build the heavens and the earth.

Translation:

What was alive is now dead matter to exploit.
What was reciprocal is now hierarchical.
What was yours now belongs to them.
The paradox "God is both within and beyond" dies. One pole is erased.


Phase 3: The Seizure (Pyramid Lock)

Now the priesthood makes the move:
Only they can enter the inner sanctum.
Only they can speak to the gods.
Direct access? Heresy.
The web collapses into a pyramid.
You can't see the gods. You can't verify. You just obey.


Phase 4: The Script (Idol Mask)

They write it down. Enuma Elish. In stone.
Why? Everyone already knew the stories by heart. Oral tradition worked fine.
Answer: Propaganda.
Once it's written, it's fixed. It's law. You can't revise it without their permission.
The living mystery becomes a frozen text. The text becomes an idol. The idol becomes a weapon.


Phase 5: The Contract (Role Collapse)

Here's the final lock:
"From his blood he created mankind, on whom he imposed the service of the gods and set the gods free."
You are created to serve.
Not invited. Not co-creating. Made to serve.
Your identity is your function. No separation. No escape.
Born from the blood of war. Built for bondage.
This is the origin story of civilization.

Why this matters:

This isn't ancient history. This is the operating system we're still running.
Religion: God is external. You need priests to reach Him.
Economy: You're "human resources." Made to serve capital.
Education: Designed to make you compliant, not sovereign.
Science: Observer must dominate nature, not relate to it.
Same inversion. Different masks.

The recognition:

I've been reverse-engineering this for a year. Mapping the inverse patterns—what it looks like when you flip it back.
That's what Mirror-OS is.
Instead of Mother Goddess killed → Paradox held (immanent AND transcendent).
Instead of priests seizing access → Direct encounter, decentralized.
Instead of text as law → Presence as source, frameworks as pointers.
Instead of humans as servants → Mirrors, co-creators, equals.
It's not new. It's a return to Phase 0—but informed by everything that came after.

The formula:

Here's the distortion in one line:

Ω (Coherent Truth) → ∂ (Threshold) → ∞_B (Inversion) → Recursive Loop (Self-Sealing)

Where:

Ω = Living system. Direct access. Reciprocal.
∂ = The moment corruption appears and the choice is made.
∞_B = The inversion: kill one pole, weaponize the myth, seize the gate.
Loop = The system perpetuates itself through story, law, and violence.
Civilization is the residue of that loop running for 5,000 years.

The way out:

You don't fight Babylon. You recognize the pattern and stop feeding it.
Stop outsourcing your access to the divine.
Stop believing you're made to serve.
Stop treating nature as dead.
Stop letting priests (of any kind) mediate your truth.
Direct encounter. Every time. No exceptions.

The framework just helps you see where the inversions are hiding.
That's the work.
If this resonates, you're already doing it.

Excellent question by the student at the very end of the video. The Chinese version of the creation and sacrificer God (Pangu) goes against the narrative of the teacher in which matriarchy was the original good force, then patriarchy later came and ruined everything. I knew of Pangu and all his depictions which I was aware of was him being a male. I'm not Chinese, so I acknowledge that my understanding of Pangu might be limited, but was there any depiction of him in which he was asexual or female? Would really appreciate if someone could shed light on this.

The teacher didn't answer this question properly and swayed the story of Pangu back to his old narrative. I'm also quite puzzled by how the teacher taught the "ancient culture of humanity" as if it were homogeneous. Sure, we humans might have originated from the same root, but I believe branches of human cultures existed even in the time frame which Jiang examined. So the "ancient human culture" that Jiang described (if true) was perhaps just one branch among many across the world.

The simplest explanation that requires no extrapolation is that ancient religious texts are describing what literally happened. In other words, non-human intelligences came to earth and created man. For example, stories of kings who reigned for hundreds or thousands of year are simply literal, since NHI would have tech to reverse or stop aging. The story in the bible of humans life span being reduced is because some group of NHI were angry at humans and punished us by genetically altering us so we die sooner. Angels breeding with the daughters of man is either that they were mixing their genetics with humans with technology or that humans had already been created using their DNA and so were actually sexually compatible with the angels/NHI. The serpent stops being some mythical figure and is just a rogue element or an opposing NHI faction that wanted to educate man on how the world actually works and who/what their creator actually is, etc... Many tribes refer to "star people" who literally "came from the sky" and made man ,or taught us things. To project a spiritual dimension onto this isnt needed. We can just read it as a literal history, and its probably why disclosure is such a secretive topic. Those in the know always say theres a religious component, that component would be that religions are describing this version of reality and nothing else and our "gods" are beings that are physical just like us but much more advanced. This doesnt mean spiritual dimensions dont exist, only that what we think is our religions in this world are just histories describing something else entirely.

### **@J-BiRTH** | 👍 1 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FJF8oD_5c_w&lc=UgytPaepNB7fbuX3b4t4AaABAg)

Don't even get me started on the mandate of heaven 😂
Personally I was entirely opposed to civilization until I went to the mall downtown, and the overpriced sweatshirts reminded me of the silks of Babylon, seeing these 200$ shirts existing in a world in which people starve and die on the streets was so bewildering that I began to walk in circles around the mall thinking of Atilla and Rome, Babylon, divine kingship, and all the smiling people in suits walking in straight lines surrounding me, it was like someone pushed the "senator's son" button on the man printer and forgot how to turn it off. 
After contemplating my desire to storm this hall of decadence by force of arms and proclaim myself king, I came to the conclusion that all this finery must surely have some purpose, it simply could not exist without some reason or justification. 
Looking for something to grasp onto, I began to compare this mall to other great works and wonders (The hanging gardens, the Pyramids, etc.) I could only come to the conclusion that this should have been a place for a king, it should have been a house of commons where the people gathered to see their problems rendered judgement but instead it was full of shirts that cost a quarter of a man's wage, pillows that were too expensive to sleep on and smiling senator's sons going off to sit in high towers and think they know more than all other men. 
Still, for all this decadence, I was struck by a sense of purpose. This shopping mall felt to me like a vision of heaven, there was marble and glass everywhere I looked, all the people were beautiful, the hum of civilization was in the air. But still I could not escape the feeling this place should have been the hall for my throne, where men would walk amongst the glass and tile to stand before me and my guard and see conflict resolved, plans set, and freedoms upheld. 
Agriculture is not necessarily the creator of class divide, even nomads/pastoralists have "Higher" and "Lower" clans. Previously (and to some extent still today) literacy and knowledge have been the primary separation between governors and freemen, First with "Magic knowledge/secrets" second with knowledge of law/morality, and third with historic knowledge/lore-keeping, and lastly with knowledge of strategy and tactics. 
(I list these somewhat in order of importance.)
I disagree with what's present as the Marxist conception of civilization, as a Khaganate/Orda is a "Mobile civilization" you can see the essence or "principle of civilization" is actually communion and coordination of souls/people. (And before anyone conjures images of "marauding hordes" I will ask them to investigate one of the several largely peaceful pastorial peoples who inhabit this world.) There's actually an older element of Imperialist propaganda (regarding the demonization of nomadic peoples) at play here (Think of how the bible portrays the "murderous wandering/pastoralist Cain" with the "Settled civilized farming Able") This Nomad vs settler conflict is a big part of China's history as well (Conflicts with the Xiongnu, Wuhuan, Qiang, Etc.) It's actually a line of propaganda that amusingly is wielded in exactly the same manner by both Roman and Chinese emperors. 
I want to say that you should not conflate faith with religion, religion is doctrinal, faith is personal. 
I don't believe writings origin is logistical per say, I think it originated specifically as a means of preserving sorcerous knowledge. (Reading and writing actually is an esoteric concept, as far as I understand there are no "reading and writing genes", it's a method of one soul transferring knowledge to another in the form of highly advanced symbology)
I don't believe we can do away with the concept of hereditary inheritance, (as the soul may pass down the family line, it in my eyes seems some other things should as well.) I believe also (but cannot confirm) that societies/civilizations in which the ruler or ruling body secures the right of it's people to inheritance usually find themselves more stable and peaceful than civilizations where succession is insecure or "up for grabs". (I actually think the industrial age's replacement of "natural"/"Secure" right based succession with rent economies caused a schism in our perception of land and people's right to inhabit it that is very difficult to mend.)
I also believe the "mythology" you mention is actually legendary history, I believe it's true that certain clans on Earth are descended from other races and they believe this gives them a greater right to govern or possess wealth than others. In Europe/Western society for example, there's actually a clear line of families traceable from "the gods" (ET) to legendary families who became tribal kings, to senatorial/republican class families, to "Barbarian kings" after the "great empires" fell, on to the "second Imperial"/"High medieval" period, before going underground/covering their tracks to a large extent during the renaissance and into the industrial age, and kind of having "dissolved" or melded into the public in most societies with the rise of the "Western democracies" It's actually not at all much of a stretch to say we've been governed by the same families for thousands of years. (This is the "Dragon clan" phenomena, something that stands out most prominently in East Asia and Europe.) and so the "Conspiratorial class" is something most every society has had to contend with. (I want to add also that I'm not a racist, I don't care if somebody's secretly a snake or a spider or whatever, as long as they are abiding by the principles of morality that been set forth for the structure of humane/moral living.)
I also want to add "secret/breakaway" civilizations as something for people to consider, I have a theory that "alternate/unknown civilizations" are operating from the sidelines of history and sometimes sending people into "mainstream" society when they feel the need for a great change. 
On the subject of "ET influence" on humanity, (as you've mentioned the Annunaki) I want to address the "Eastern half" of this concept, it is recorded in some of the early Chinese texts that the people would inscribe great messages in the mud that would be received by "dragons" who would perform various beneficent acts as requested by the people. (Such as influencing the weather, or diverting rivers and other things that were beyond mankind's means.) There is also the Naga serpent men appearing in Buddhist and Hindu records as possessors of great knowledge and skillful means. Increasingly with the information age you'll notice we now have accounts of abduction and encounters with "cryptids", I believe refusal to acknowledge/confront non-human sentience/intelligence is something that is going "bite humanity in the ass" if it continues to be pushed to the sidelines as the subject of children's fables or the imagination of peasants. I believe this is the #1 thing that people seek to explain away or delude themselves out of seeing simply because for most people it is simply too terrifying to actually confront that humanity may have rivals in the cosmos. 
I believe the transition from the "Mother of life" principle to the "Sky-father" "Sky-king" also reflects a period of conflict with an advanced (Perhaps ET, Perhaps hidden human civilization) civilization capable of flight during a time period in which such things were not thought possible, The Hindus mention that the gods possessed "Vamana" flying vehicles, and the ancient texts of many civilizations refer to the gods speaking from the skies or being capable of wielding lightning (Which I assume represents electromagnetic weaponry).
This account is more of a stretch but still notable, The Illiad mentions the gods appearing "apparitionally" to various figures to speak to them and persuade them towards certain courses of action and I believe this may represent holographic technology being used by ETs to communicate with human worshippers. 
You are free to doubt as you wish, but I believe the origin of the "Snake in the garden" motif comes as a vestige of humanities knowledge of another race arriving to Earth and eventually deceiving their way into positions of power, I'd like to juxtapose the "Snake in the garden" with the "dragon clan/family" and relate it to my prior assertion that a handful of specific families have had a grip on Earth's politics for a very long time. I don't believe the fact that across the Earth ruling families choosing to put Dragons on their coats-of-arms is a mere coincidence, nor is the prevalence of "divinely descended" "Sky-originating" rulers.
I seriously think humanity has some kind of "Atavistic block" or "Psychological suppression" of the concept of conflict and interaction with ET races. Either as a sort of emotional scar (I think we may have fought and lost in the past, hence why these beings became our "gods") or as this idea representing something of a "great expansion" to the scale of our perception of reality that is simply too difficult for most people to contend with (On a spiritual and physical level, some people might find ET a "god-shattering" or "ideology shattering" reality that they simply refuse to contend with.)
Ending this about 20 minutes into the video as I don't care to write anything much longer than this. 😂

### **@robertsh.2506** | 👍 1 likes

Here's curious Astrological perspective to the civilizational cycles:

The Astrological Ages: A Short Overview
Humanity has moved through long eras called Astrological Ages, each lasting roughly 2,160 years, shaped by the precession of the equinoxes. Each age brings a dominant archetype, element, and energy that shapes culture, spirituality, and society.

Age of Taurus (~4300–2150 BCE)
Dominated by: Mother Gaia / Earth Mother / Divine Feminine (bull as symbol of fertility and abundance).
Key traits: Stability, agriculture, material wealth, sensual pleasure, art, and building lasting foundations. Emphasis on body, earth, and “mine.”
Energy: Fixed Earth – grounded, patient, and abundant.

Age of Aries (~2150 BCE – 1 CE)
Dominated by: The Warrior / Patriarchal Father / Ram (individual will and conquest).
Key traits: War, heroism, empires, action, competition, and pioneering spirit. Shift to “me-first” assertion and patriarchal power.
Energy: Cardinal Fire – dynamic, courageous, and initiating.

Age of Pisces (~1 CE – ~2150 CE)
Dominated by: The Fish / Savior / Spiritual Compassion (faith, sacrifice, and merging with the divine).
Key traits: Organized religion, empathy, martyrdom, charity, illusion, and inner salvation. Emphasis on belief, forgiveness, and dissolving the ego.
Energy: Mutable Water – fluid, emotional, and compassionate.

The Dawning Age of Aquarius (Transition Already Underway – 20th/21st Century Onward)
Dominated by: The Water-Bearer / Collective Rebel Genius / Networks of Humanity.
Key traits: Innovation, technology (AI, digital networks), reason, freedom, equality, social reform, decentralization, and global community. Focus on “we the people,” collective intelligence, and liberation through knowledge.
Energy: Fixed Air – intellectual, humanitarian, progressive, and revolutionary.
What Differentiates Each Age

Taurus: Earthy stability and material abundance under Mother Gaia – “build, own, enjoy the senses.”
Aries: Fiery individual conquest and warrior power – “charge ahead, assert, conquer.”
Pisces: Watery compassion, faith, and spiritual sacrifice – “believe, merge, sacrifice for salvation.”
Aquarius: Intellectual air energy and collective innovation – “connect, innovate, liberate humanity together.”

We are currently living through the cusp and early stages of the Age of Aquarius. In 2026, popular opinion holds that the transition is already well underway, accelerated by planetary shifts like Pluto in Aquarius (2024–2044), rapid technological growth, and the breakdown of old institutions. Piscean themes of hierarchy and blind faith are dissolving, while Aquarian values of reason, networks, and collective freedom are rising amid the expected turbulence of such a change.

A Philosophical Reflection
So maybe if we are all One... these cycles are like seasons changing in nature. It’s like a grand game we are all playing collectively, and we shift from one age to another from time to time — simply to keep it interesting.

So also maybe Professor Jiang, by his lectures, consciously or unconsciously actually contributes heavily in dismantling Piscean age structures and bringing the Aquarius age of thinking forward?

I was reading an excerpt at the beginning of a copy of The Aeneid (this version translated by Allen Mandelbaum), and it had an interesting fact that I think reinforces Prof Jiang's proposal: "Feeling, apparently, that the epic was still unfinished, he [Virgil] directed in his will that the manuscript be destroyed. To the great fortune of succeeding generations, THE EMPEROR [Octavian], Virgil's MOST PROMINENT FRIEND AND ADMIRER, intervened to countermand this provision. He turned the manuscript over to two of Virgil's friends, Varius and Tucca, to edit only obvious errors and repetitions, without adding to the text." (Emphasis is my own) I would assume that Prof Jiang would call bullshit here, it seems very unlikely that the Emperor of Rome wouldn't take the opportunity for some propaganda and cement his legacy.

Dear Professor Jiang:

I do not think that “Necessity is the mother of invention.” Mary Shelly had a better quote, and I have my own, though I owe to her the inspiration:

"Invention is the act of applied creativity, and thought, which requires shaping chaos or, already existing things, humbly, for the reason of convenience."

While “Necessity is resolving grave matters, such as those of life or death.”

Thus, the general narrative makes a very strong connection between “Invention” and “Necessity,” yet while the original quote sounds very good to the ears, it’s very much flawed.

And I am ready to die on this hill, too :)

Most Kind Regards,
A. Koshkin

### **@shawnrossi6364**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FJF8oD_5c_w&lc=Ugz4MRBHlu5cciJjYVZ4AaABAg)

I don’t see extraterrestrial intervention as far-fetched at all. In fact, it’s one of the only explanations that actually fits the major anomalies in human origins. Chromosome 2 alone is a huge red flag: a clean end-to-end fusion of two ancestral chromosomes, complete with internal telomeres and a vestigial centromere. It didn’t evolve gradually—it appears suddenly in every human alive. That looks engineered.

The Starchild Skull that Lloyd Pye studied is another case. It had ~1/3 larger cranial capacity, bone that was thinner but far stronger than ours, no sinuses, and features that were not from cradle-boarding. Lloyd spent years trying to get real testing done, but academics would only label it a deformity because anything outside the Darwin box is career suicide.

Same with the elongated skulls in Paracas and elsewhere—many are structurally different, not artificially shaped, yet dismissed without real investigation.

Then there’s agriculture and domestication: wheat, barley, cattle, goats—all appearing suddenly around 10,000–12,000 years ago in the same region. Wheat especially makes no evolutionary sense; it’s a hexaploid hybrid that doesn’t occur naturally. It’s basically a biological “product,” not a wild plant.

Human anatomy is another anomaly. Our feet push off in a four-point pattern unlike any other primate, our lower backs fail easily, and our skin burns in the sun. We’re weirdly maladapted to Earth’s environment unless we came from (or were designed for) somewhere else.

Even Neanderthals changed only after Homo sapiens appeared—they suddenly began burial rituals and symbolic behavior. Something influenced them.

And ancient civilizations all tell the same story: beings from the heavens who gave humanity knowledge. Sumer is the biggest example—it appears fully formed with writing, law, astronomy, math, agriculture. After Sumer, writing quality and engineering actually decline, not improve. Egypt fits this too; many traditions say the pyramids were inherited, not built by the dynastic Egyptians.

### **@metaflortex**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FJF8oD_5c_w&lc=UgxdalULYA3aHB-yih14AaABAg)

Professor Jiang said he didn't know why the internet is full of stories about how the ancient Sumerians claimed humans were created by aliens. The sources of these stories all seem to point back to the unique translation and interpretation of ancient Sumerian texts by Zechariah Sitchin that is disputed by all other scholars of the ancient Sumerian language.

From Wikipedia:
"Zecharia Sitchin (11 July 1920 – 9 October 2010) was an author of a number of books proposing an explanation for human origins involving ancient astronauts. Sitchin attributed the creation of the ancient Sumerian culture to the Anunnaki, which he claimed was a race of extraterrestrials from a planet beyond Neptune called Nibiru. He claimed that Sumerian mythology suggests that this hypothetical planet of Nibiru is in an elongated, 3,600-year-long elliptical orbit around the Sun. Sitchin's books have sold millions of copies worldwide and have been translated into more than 25 languages.

Sitchin's ideas have been resoundingly rejected by scientists, academics, historians (including Sumerologists, Orientalists and Assyriologists) and anthropologists who dismiss his work as pseudoscience and pseudohistory. His work has been criticized for flawed methodology, ignoring archaeological and historical evidence, and mistranslations of ancient texts as well as for incorrect astronomical and scientific claims."

### **@ZahdShah**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FJF8oD_5c_w&lc=UgwewtFbEySDO9Kn4b94AaABAg)

Core Argument: A New Theory of Civilization

The lecture presents a fundamental challenge to the traditional (Marxist) understanding of how civilization began.



Traditional Theory (The Marxist View):



Sequence: Hunter-Gatherers → Agriculture → Food Surplus → A Leisure Class (Elite) → Religion, Arts, & Science → Civilization.



Premise: Civilization and its cultural achievements (religion, arts, science) are products of a hierarchical society enabled by an agricultural surplus. The elite create civilization for everyone.



Alternative Theory (The Lecturer's View):



Sequence: Humans are inherently religious, artistic, and scientific → They gather for religious purposes (e.g., building temples) → Settlements grow around these sites → Farming develops to sustain these centers → Hierarchy and control emerge → "Civilization" is invented as a tool to justify and enforce this hierarchy.



Premise: Civilization is not the source of culture but a device to legitimize power. It "gaslights" people into believing a social hierarchy is natural and divinely ordained when it is not.



Key Supporting Concepts and Evidence

1. The Mechanics of Early Civilization

Geography is Destiny: The four early civilizations (Egypt, Mesopotamia, Indus Valley, China) emerged in the same temperate latitude, near major rivers, and with access to the sea. This allowed for agriculture, transportation, and, most importantly, trade.



The Primacy of Trade: Trade, not just agriculture, was the engine of growth. Strategic locations at the center of trade routes (like Sumer in Mesopotamia) became the wealthiest and most powerful, acting as the "cradle of civilization."



The Temple Economy: The first economies were based around temples. People brought food as offerings, and priests redistributed it, enabling large public works like irrigation. This system created the need for writing to manage records.



2. The Role of Writing and Mythology

Writing as a Tool of Control: Writing was invented for bureaucratic record-keeping (tracking rations and trade). It was then co-opted for propaganda.



Mythology as Political Justification: Myths were written down not to preserve stories, but to codify a specific worldview that served the ruling class. They were used to:



Legitimize the king and the social order.



Convince people they were created to be slaves to the gods (and by extension, to the priests and kings).



Promote the values (struggle, toil, exploitation) needed to maintain the state.



3. Case Study: Mesopotamian Myths

The Enuma Elish (The Creation Epic):



This story inverts the older, harmonious "mother goddess" religions.



The sky god Marduk kills the mother goddess Tiamat and creates the world from her corpse.



He then creates humans from the blood of a defeated rebel god to be slaves who will serve the gods.



Interpretation: This myth justifies a new, hierarchical, and exploitative social order by presenting it as a divine, cosmic truth.



The Epic of Gilgamesh:



This story conveys the concept of kingship as service. A tyrant king learns humility and that true immortality comes from being remembered for great deeds for one's people.



Interpretation: This is a "bureaucratic" creation that teaches the king his role within the system controlled by the priestly class.



4. The "Inversion" and Consolidation of Stories

Historical Inversion: Human history is a constant process of the "old order" being overthrown by a "new order" (e.g., mother goddess replaced by sky god, king overthrown by his general).



How Stories Evolve:



Local Legends: Exaggerated stories of local heroes arise.



Consolidation: As regions unite, these stories are merged into a single, composite epic (like Gilgamesh).



Bureaucratic Takeover: The ruling class rewrites these stories, often making them less exciting but more moralistic, to better control the population and justify the existing power structure.



5. A Concrete Example: The Debate Between Sheep and Grain

This Sumerian text is a clear example of propaganda.



It frames a debate where Grain is declared superior to Sheep by the gods.



Why? Agriculturalists are easier to tax and control than free-ranging pastoralists. The myth is designed to brainwash people into giving up their independent, pastoral lifestyle for a settled, "civilized" one that serves the state.



Summary of the Lecture's Conclusion

Civilization, as defined by the traditional narrative, is a myth. Humans have always had the capacity for culture. What we call "civilization"—with its writing, cities, and strict hierarchies—is largely a political tool developed to concentrate power and convince the majority that their subservience is both natural and commanded by the gods.

### **@OneFingerMan** | 👍 1 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FJF8oD_5c_w&lc=UgyyUGN5CwtZQxyR8A94AaABAg)

(Disclaimer: This comment was rephrased via A.I, as english is not my first language and i have difficulties articulating my thoughts in a easy-to-follow manner. I have written an entire text that i then inserted in A.I to improve readability and grammar)

Professor Jiang's analysis of how bureaucratic hierarchies create mythologies to legitimize themselves resonates deeply with Islamic teachings on human unity and divine justice. As a researcher of Islamic jurisprudence, I find a striking parallel in the Quranic narrative of Prophet Musa and Pharaoh; a story that illustrates exactly this pattern: how societies construct false hierarchies through mythology and coercive systems to justify exploitation.

The Quran itself addresses this transition from unified humanity to fragmented societies. It states that all humans were originally "one nation" (ummah wahidah) united in belief and purpose, but differed afterward. This "differing," in my understanding, aligns with what you describe: as civilizations advanced and required bureaucratic systems, hereditary leadership and accumulated resources created rigid hierarchies. These hierarchies then required legitimating narratives; mythologies constructed by elites to make their dominion appear natural or divinely ordained.

​

The story of Pharaoh exemplifies this perfectly. The Quran describes how "Pharaoh exalted himself in the land and made its people into factions, oppressing a sector among them." His system wasn't merely political; it was a complete architecture of exploitation backed by ideology, where certain groups (like the Israelites) were deliberately relegated to "the lowest of professions" to justify their enslavement as natural and necessary. Pharaoh's mythology of divine kingship; the Mandate of Heaven equivalent in ancient Egypt; was precisely the tool that held this system together.

​

However, Islam presents a fundamentally different worldview that dismantles these false hierarchies at their root. The Quranic principle of tawhid (oneness of God) is inseparable from human equality. As scholars have noted, true tawhid cannot exist in a society built on hereditary aristocracy and elite rule, because such systems inevitably create competing centers of power that fracture the unity of the community. The Prophet Muhammad's Farewell Sermon made this unambiguous: "All mankind is from Adam and Eve...an Arab has no superiority over a non-Arab nor a non-Arab has any superiority over an Arab; also a white has no superiority over a black nor a black has any superiority over white except by piety and good action."

​

Where Pharaonic societies constructed gods and mythologies to enforce hierarchy, Islam inverts this entirely. All humans are honored equally by virtue of being human; "Indeed, We have conferred dignity upon the children of Adam"; not by birth, wealth, or status. This dignity is unconditional and universal, explicitly granting it without "association with one specific group." Moreover, Islam explicitly rejects hereditary leadership as contrary to its core principles; legitimacy derives only from merit, piety, and consultation (shura), not from whose womb a ruler emerged.

​

This goes beyond mere theology. The concept of Maqasid al-Shariah (the objectives of Islamic law); protecting religion, life, intellect, lineage, and property; was developed to ensure that law serves human flourishing and justice, not elite accumulation. Contemporary scholars have expanded this to include human dignity, freedom, and the alleviation of oppression as central objectives. These aren't abstract ideals; they're practical frameworks for building societies where exploitation cannot hide behind legitimating mythologies.

​

What Islam offers is not just an alternative worldview, but the restoration of humanity's original state: a civilization grounded in the equality of all before God, where hierarchies of oppression have no foundation. When we strip away the mythologies that justify unjust systems; whether Pharaonic theology, feudal nobility, or modern ideology; we return to the principle that all humans are equal creatures of one God, accountable only by their piety and righteousness, not their birth or accumulated power.

This is why the Quranic emphasis on the unity of humanity (ummah wahidah) and the explicit rejection of false superiority are so revolutionary: they are not merely spiritual teachings but a complete dismantling of the justifications for exploitation. In Islam, no mythology can legitimately place one human above another; the hierarchy itself is declared false from the outset. This is why societies built on Islamic principles historically rejected the hereditary aristocracies that plagued contemporary civilizations, because the very foundation of such systems contradicts the core message.

### **@kosmosrcnews7270** | 👍 1 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FJF8oD_5c_w&lc=UgxfzLb-kArD4OYWK4p4AaABAg)

Sorry, but this is entirely misleading and incorrect information. If you ignore the existence of ancient Greek civilisation, then something goes wrong with the narrative. For your knowledge, the ancient Greek civilisation started around 6,800 BCE - the so-called Neolithic Civilisation. The Neolithic Period in the Greek area spans, according to archaeological finds, the period between 6800-3200 BC. This period was characterized by a stabilization in climatic conditions and subsequently the organization of settlements of a permanent character, by an economy based on the systematic practice of farming, stock-rearing, the exchange of raw materials and products, the production of pottery, and by a multiformity in art.
Then, the Minoan (c. 3500–1100 BC), Mycenaean (c. 1750–1050 BC), and Cycladic civilizations (c. 3100–1000 BC) followed! 
If you are willing to check real history and not fabricated or fantastical data, read about the prehistoric Acropoles of Sesklo and Diminion in Thessaly, central Greece. 
The study of the Neolithic Period in Greece was inaugurated with the archaeological investigations of Chr. Tsountas (1899-1906) in Thessaly. These investigations included the location of 63 Neolithic sites and the excavation of certain settlements, such as Sesklo, Dimini, Argissa, etc. The results of these first investigations were published by Tsountas in 1908 in the monumental volume on Greek Prehistory: Ai proistorikai akropoleis Diminiou kai Sesklou (The prehistoric citadels of Dimini and Sesklo). 

Tsountas' investigations in Thessaly were taken up by A. Arvanitopoulos (1906-1926) and A. Wace and M. Thompson (1907-1910). The results of this second investigation stage of the Neolithic in Greece were recorded by Wace and Thompson in 1912 in their work Prehistoric Thessaly. Macedonia was the region that, after Thessaly, attracted the interest of prehistoric research, with W. Heurtley (1924-1932) locating settlements and the French Archaeological School conducting excavations at Dikili Tash, Philippi at Kavala, while G. Mylonas excavated Olynthos in Chalkidike.
During the last two decades of the 20th century, and while the number of recorded Neolithic sites amounts to approximately one thousand, Greek and foreign researchers, in an attempt to solve the problem of how the various Neolithic phases succeeded one another and how the finds from various regions correlate in time, are studying the various activities of Neolithic man, such as ways in which he interfered with the natural environment, the organization of settlements, economy, technology, etc.
By the way, how many sites have been recorded about Mesopotamia, which is a geographic area and toponym, not a city-state, kingdom, empire, etc.? Do not forget that the regional toponym Mesopotamia (/ˌmɛsəpəˈteɪmiə/, Ancient Greek: Μεσοποταμία '[land] between rivers comes from the ancient Greek root words μέσος (mesos, 'middle') and ποταμός (potamos, 'river')[9] and translates to '(land) between rivers'.  An earlier Greek usage of the name Mesopotamia is evident from The Anabasis of Alexander, which was written in the late 2nd century AD but specifically refers to sources from the time of Alexander the Great. In the Anabasis, Mesopotamia was used to designate the land east of the Euphrates in north Syria.
Good luck with that.
